### WP-36: Load the context-menu wizard on demand (UD-13 changed)

| Release | Size | Points | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1.0 (M2) | M | 4 | WP-35 |

**Scope**

UD-13 (owner): the context menu no longer carries the wizard template and field data inline. It renders only the wizard entry (same position and wording as now) with the data needed to ask for the body; opening the wizard fetches the body from a new GET action of `ContextMenuWizardController` (same origin, `X-Requested-With`, HTML partial, never `.json`/`.xml` API formats), which applies exactly the authorization and selection rules of the context menu today (login, every issue visible, `SelectionGraph`, `ParentDetector`, `issue.editable_custom_fields(user)` for every selected issue, fail closed, hidden fields never in the response, UD-37). The save route is unchanged. Loading state, an error message on failure (no hidden field named), a second open reuses or refetches safely, Escape and Cancel as now, CSRF token present in the fetched form. The S1 budget moves from the menu to the fetched body; the menu stays small for any list size.

**Files**

- `app/controllers/context_menu_wizard_controller.rb`, `config/routes.rb`, `lib/redmine_depending_custom_fields/hooks/context_menu_hook.rb`, `app/helpers/context_menu_wizard_helper.rb`, `app/views/depending_custom_fields/_context_menu_wizard.html.erb`
- `assets/javascripts/context_menu_wizard.js`, `assets/stylesheets/depending_custom_fields.css`
- specs (requests, context menu, system, jsdom), `CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md`, docs/specs/*.md

**Tests**

- Permission matrix on the new action (anonymous, non-member, member without edit rights, issue not visible, mixed selection); hidden fields absent; the menu body size independent of the list size; the fetched body equals today's wizard for the same selection; system specs on 5.1 and 7.0 for open, choose, save, cancel and a failed fetch

**Acceptance**

- Standard gate green on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0; npm test; system specs on 5.1 and 7.0

**Open input from WP-34**: (1) Since WP-34 (UD-10 changed by the owner) `DependingFormatMethods#bulk_edit_tag` is core's `List#bulk_edit_tag` plus the bulk attributes for every field, so a required depending field of the wizard has no '(none)' option; `test/js/fixtures/markup/wizard_template.html` is unchanged by WP-34 (it has no required field) and `runtime_wizard.test.js` W2 removes the middle field's '(none)' in a tweak. The fetched body must keep this: no `__none__` and no `data-dcf-required-none` for required fields (the markup fixture contract refuses the attribute anywhere). (2) In the wizard the clearing cascade stops at a required field: it stays on '(No change)' with the empty hint and the fields below it read it as unchanged; the save then answers per issue (a kept disallowed value refused 'is invalid' with 422, a blank one saved), pinned in `spec/requests/bulk_update_dependencies_spec.rb` 'answers the wizard save of a required ... child on (No change) issue by issue'. (3) `ContextMenuWizardController`'s '__none__' to '' mapping is unchanged and still serves the optional fields' core '(none)'.

**Related decisions and defects**: UD-11, UD-13, UD-37, SD-01

**Delivered (decisions taken during implementation)**

- Route: `get 'depending_custom_fields/wizard(.:format)', to: 'context_menu_wizard#show', as: 'depending_custom_fields_wizard'`, the first line of `config/routes.rb`, so `depending_custom_fields/:id` (API show, format json) never sees the path. The optional format is matched only so that the action refuses it: `prepend_before_action :refuse_format` answers 404 to any extension (`.json`, `.xml`, `.html`, `.js`) before Redmine's own callbacks, so neither a session nor an API key is read and the API show never runs (`expect_any_instance_of(DependingCustomFieldsApiController).not_to receive(:show)`).
- `ContextMenuWizardController#show`: `require_login` (no `accept_api_auth`: `key=` and `X-Redmine-API-Key` never authenticate, anonymous browser GET redirects to `/login`, anonymous XHR 401 also when the anonymous role may edit), then the menu's rules: ids as core sends them (`ids[]`) or a comma string; every element a String of canonical digits (`DependencyRules::CANONICAL_ID`, at most 10 characters and 2,147,483,647), deduplicated, else the generic 404 (a Hash, an Array element that is a Hash, '0', '-1', '1;2', blanks, a 20-digit number, a script payload); `Issue.where(id:).preload(:project, :tracker, :status)` in database order (as core find_issues: none found 404, an invisible issue 403, unknown ids next to known ones ignored); `User.current.allowed_to?(:edit_issues, issue.project)` for every issue (the ContextMenuHook gate, 403: closed project, archived project, module disabled, member without edit, non-member, mixed selection); one `SelectionGraph.new(issues)`, `ParentDetector.for_issues(issues, graph:, user:)`, the requested `parent_id` (canonical digits) must be one of those roots and `ParentDetector.wizard_levels` non-empty, else the same generic 404 (core `render_404`; a hidden root, a child, an unknown field and a root without levels give the same status and the same body). Any `StandardError` is logged as `[DCF] context menu wizard failed: <class>` and answered `head :internal_server_error` (no text). `prepend_before_action :no_store` sets `Cache-Control: no-store` on every answer of the action (200, 403, 404, 500, the login redirect); the GET writes nothing (Issue, CustomValue, Journal and JournalDetail counts and `updated_on` unchanged).
- Body: `_context_menu_wizard_form.html.erb` (locals `parent_field`, `issues`, `fields_hierarchy`) rendered `formats: [:html], layout: false, content_type: 'text/html'`; the form lines are those of the former template, byte for byte, plus one line `<button type="button" class="cf-wizard-cancel"><%= l(:button_cancel) %></button>` in `.cf-actions` (core label, no new key). Golden evidence (a temporary fixed-id spec, removed afterwards): the old template content captured on the unchanged code (`7.0-rspec-subset-20261105-072355`) and the new GET body (`7.0-rspec-subset-20261105-073225`) are equal apart from that Cancel line, for the 3-field chain and for a required middle field (2 `__none__` options, none on the required field, no `data-dcf-required-none`); the regenerated `wizard_body.html` equals the form of the former `wizard_template.html` plus the Cancel line.
- Menu: `_context_menu_wizard.html.erb` renders the entry only, with the same gate and decision (`wizard_levels` non-empty for the gate user), position and wording, and `data-wizard-url="<%= depending_custom_fields_wizard_path %>"` (relative URL root included); `data-template-id` is gone from the li and the link; no template, form, select or option. ContextMenuHook is unchanged (one graph per menu request, reused from the patch).
- JS (`context_menu_wizard.js`, ES2017, no innerHTML, no eval): a click shows `p.cf-wizard-loading[role=status]` with `aria-busy` at once (meta key `wizardLoading`), resolves `getAttribute('data-wizard-url')` against the page URL (same origin only, the SP-15 check now shared with the save action as `sameOrigin`), builds `parent_id` and `ids[]` with `URLSearchParams`, fetches `GET` with `credentials: 'same-origin'`, `cache: 'no-store'`, `X-Requested-With`, `Accept: text/html` and an `AbortController`; success is `res.ok && !res.redirected` with a `text/html` type; the text is parsed with `DOMParser`, exactly one form that is `form.cf-wizard-form` is accepted, its scripts and `on*` attributes are removed and it is imported with `importNode`, then `init(container)` runs synchronously and the container is positioned again. Any failure (rejection, abort, any non-ok status, a followed redirect, another type, no or two forms, a failing `text()`) shows one `p.cf-wizard-error[role=alert]` with the meta key `wizardLoadFailed`, never server text and nothing on the console; a later click asks again. Each open closes the previous state first (abort, listeners removed), so a second click refetches; a late answer for a closed, replaced or disconnected entry is dropped. Escape (document keydown) and the Cancel button close and give the focus back to the entry link; after the load the focus moves to the first select. Outside mousedown and leaving the entry close as before (no refocus).
- Texts: core `label_loading` ('Loading...', present in 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 in all four locales; review round 1, was the plugin key `text_dcf_wizard_loading`) and the new `text_dcf_wizard_load_failed` ('The wizard could not be loaded.') in en, de, fr, nl, `ClientConfig::I18N` (13 entries), the runtime fallback map and the wizard's own fallbacks; registry rows, totals, section 5 and section 8.3; the four head fixtures regenerated.
- Fixtures: `wizard_template.html` replaced by the generated `wizard_entry.html` (the menu entry) and `wizard_body.html` (the GET response); every consumer adapted (markup fixtures spec scenarios and checks, `contract_fixtures.test.js`, `runtime_bulk.test.js`, `runtime_wizard.test.js`, `head_fixtures.test.js`); the contract checks still refuse `data-dcf-required-none`, assert 3 selects, implicit labels, `data-dcf-wizard-field` and no `option[selected]`.
- Size (S1, `:large`): `DCF BUDGET context menu S1: 6144 bytes, entry 190 bytes; wizard body 421703 bytes, selects 420494 bytes` (7.0); bounds: the entry at most 1 KiB, the menu at most the menu of a 1-value child plus 1 KiB, the body at most 512 KiB and at most its selects plus 16 KiB, 2 selects, 5,570 non-blank options, no `context menu wizard failed` warning.
- Decisions on the open questions (the recommended or most conservative option): (1) Escape and a Cancel button are added (new behaviour; Cancel reuses core `button_cancel`, server-rendered in the body). (2) The load failure is shown inside the open container (`role=alert`), save errors keep `window.alert`. (3) The server does not require `X-Requested-With` (a plain navigation gets the same rules; the script sends it, so an expired session answers 401 instead of a followed redirect). (4) Unknown ids next to known ones are ignored, as core find_issues; none found is 404. (5) No upper bound besides the URL length (the core menu's own limit); 2,000 duplicates are answered with one issue. (6) A parent not offered is 404, the same body as a missing one. (7) Malformed `parent_id` or ids are 404 before any lookup. (8) The ETag that Rack::ETag adds to a 200 is kept (it ignores no-store and cannot be removed without a middleware change); the body holds a per-request masked token, so it never repeats and no 304 is answered (spec 'never answers 304'; mutation (d) made the bodies equal and that example failed).
- Flips and changed examples (each annotated 'Changed by WP-36' or 'Flipped by WP-36'): characterization `spec/characterization/context_menu_spec.rb` helper `wizard_levels` (fetches the body for the entry; the expectations unchanged); `spec/requests/context_menu_spec.rb` helpers `wizard_levels`, `select_names`, `form_of`, examples 'checks the wizard levels ...' (twice), 'keeps the entry markup with the wizard URL ...' (was 'keeps the entry and form markup ...'), 'escapes ...', 'offers only fields core lets ...', 'never names a dependent field hidden ...', 'calls neither Rails.cache ...' (body too), the 'context-menu code' file list (form partial and controller added) and a new static example; `context_menu_size_budget_spec.rb` (rewritten for menu and body); `context_menu_wizard_save_spec.rb`, `issue_form_contract_spec.rb`, `bulk_update_dependencies_spec.rb` (the form of the fetched body); `dcf_time_entry_hidden_children_spec.rb` (two wizard routes); `client_data_spec.rb` (`templateId` gone); `client_config_spec.rb` and `dependency_editor_config_spec.rb` (13 keys); `context_menu_wizard_controller_spec.rb` (actions save and show); markup fixture scenarios; jsdom W1 (checks inside the init wrapper), W3 about:blank (refused wizard URL, no form), X6 (wizard's own init counted), the 13-key i18n example, `runtime_bulk.test.js` 2 wizard examples, `contract_fixtures.test.js` (37 files), `head_fixtures.test.js` (one GET answered).
- New specs: `spec/requests/context_menu_wizard_body_spec.rb` (31 examples since review round 1: route, body, permission matrix incl. own-issues visibility, authentication, API key and formats, CSRF, parameters, hidden fields, fail closed, no-store, no 304), routing example, 'menu entry size', the L1 to L7 jsdom rows, system examples 'shows a loading state, then closes with Cancel and with Escape without saving (WP-36)' and 'shows a generic message when the body cannot be loaded, and a second click retries (WP-36)'; screenshots `tmp/capybara/dcf/wp-36/` (`wizard-open`, `wizard-loading`, `wizard-load-failed` per version).
- Docs: CHANGELOG line (Changed, last of its group) word for word in section 3 (appended to the M3 list) and 'Unreleased (0.1.0)'; PC-80; UN-72 and BC-14 'UN-01..UN-72'; UD-13 status and table row, D4; README 'Context menu wizard'; frontend design 7 item 2, fixture list and 3.6 item 10; server design 7.6 and 7.7; quality protocol context menu cost line; i18n registry.
- Red first (7.0, specs and tests written before the production change): `7.0-rspec-subset-20261105-072440` `339 examples, 59 failures` for the new and changed request, routing, controller, client, characterization, fixture and size examples (27 of the 29 body examples written then; the two 404 examples passed vacuously, the old route sent the GET to the API show, which answers 404; the 30th, 'answers 403 for an issue the member cannot see although the role may edit issues (own issues only)', was added after the production change, when the visibility mutation (a) needed a case that the edit gate does not already refuse, and was not run red); `npm test` with the unchanged `context_menu_wizard.js` `# tests 576 # pass 545 # fail 31` (W1 to W6, X6, L1 to L7, the head example and the 13-key i18n example); `7.0-system-20261105-072750` `29 examples, 2 failures` (the two new WP-36 system examples; the routes and partials of the unchanged code were restored for that run and put back afterwards).

- Review round 1 (fixer, light review with a security reviewer; findings quality-r1-1, quality-r1-2, nits quality-r1-3, quality-r1-4): quality-r1-1, 'fails closed on any error ...' asserts exactly a 500 with a blank body (was any status from 400 on); quality-r1-2, new example 'runs the same number of queries for 2 and for 20 selected issues of one project and tracker' (a member with view and edit rights, one warm-up request, then `dcf_count_queries` for 2 and 20 issues, equal counts); quality-r1-4, `show` sorts the loaded issues by the requested order (`ids.each_with_index.to_h`), so `ids[]`, `issue_ids` and `data-issue-ids` of the body follow the entry's `data-issue-ids`, and 'lists the resolved issue ids ... in the requested order' asserts the order for a request of three issues in the order 3, 1, 2 (was `match_array`); quality-r1-3, the loading text reuses core `label_loading` (`ClientConfig::I18N` 'wizardLoading' => `:label_loading`; present in 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 in en, de, fr and nl), `text_dcf_wizard_loading` is removed from the four locales and the registry (section 3 row, 4.2 row, totals 11 in M2 and 132 in all, section 5, section 6 row count, section 8.3 row); the de head fixture regenerated ('Lade...', was 'Wird geladen...'); `client_config_spec.rb` reads core's locale table under the plugin's for the locale check and gains 'defines no plugin key for the wizard loading text'; the `runtime_wiring.test.js` fallback example compares `wizardLoading` with the en head fixture and asserts no registry row for the old key. The English fallbacks 'Loading...' in `depending_custom_fields.js` and `context_menu_wizard.js` stay (the finding also proposed removing the JS defaults; without them a page without a valid meta tag would show no loading text, and the registry rule keeps a fallback for every meta key); they equal core en `label_loading`. ### WP-37: Side-by-side comparison with main: behaviour, speed and server memory (owner, 2026-11-03)

| Release | Size | Points | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1.0 (M3) | M | 1 to 9 | WP-36 and the final reviews |

**Scope**

Owner request: users must never get a slow Redmine because of this plugin. Compare the plugin from `origin/main` (0.0.15) with this branch side by side, on the same Redmine checkouts (5.1 and 7.0; 6.0 and 6.1 where main supports them) and the same data: a small field set, a medium one, and the large lists of the plan (S1 27 x 5,570 list links, S2 25,000 enumerations, the context-menu and bulk-edit cases).

- Behaviour: the same scenarios in Chromium on both (issue new, edit, show; bulk edit; context menu and wizard; admin custom field form; project values and dependency pages; saves and refusals), screenshots side by side, every difference listed as intended (CHANGELOG line) or as a defect.
- Server speed: response time per scenario (median and p95 of repeated warm requests, plus the first cold request), SQL query count and SQL time, view rendering time, HTML and JSON response size.
- Server memory: Ruby object allocations and retained memory per request (GC stats, memory_profiler or an equivalent available on the checkout) and the worker RSS before and after a series of requests, to find leaks and per-request growth.
- Browser: page load, script execution and long tasks, and the JS heap for the issue form and the editors with the large lists.
- Report in `docs/perf/` with the method, the machine, the raw numbers and tables per scenario and version; any scenario where the branch is slower than main beyond measurement noise, or uses clearly more memory, is a defect to fix (or a recorded owner decision) before the release. The goal is equal or better everywhere and much faster for large lists.

**Acceptance**

- Report committed; every regression fixed or decided by the owner; no change to behaviour that is not a CHANGELOG line.

**Open input from WP-36**: (1) WP-37 compares with main, where the menu carried the wizard body: measure the menu (`POST /issues/context_menu`, S1 6,144 bytes on 7.0 since WP-36) and the body fetched on open (`GET /depending_custom_fields/wizard`, S1 421,703 bytes) separately, and the extra round trip when the wizard opens. (2) The 0.1.0 Changed line of PC-32 ('Context-menu responses include dependency data for the wizard fields and can be larger for very big mappings.') is an earlier line kept word for word; since WP-36 it describes the body fetched on open, not the menu. Decide whether to reword it (section 3, the CHANGELOG and PC-32 together, then rerun G9). (3) Rack::ETag adds a weak ETag to the 200 body although the action sends `Cache-Control: no-store` (Rack ignores no-store); the masked CSRF token makes every body differ, so no 304 is possible; removing the header would need a middleware change. (4) The server does not require `X-Requested-With`; a plain navigation to the URL gets the bare form without layout under the same rules (decision of open question 3). (5) The GET carries `ids[]` in the query string like the core menu's own GET, so a very large selection hits the same URL length limits (about 8 KB at common proxies) for both.

**Definition of done**: the per-WP report of [`large_lists_quality_protocol.md`](large_lists_quality_protocol.md) (sections 1 to 9) with observed evidence for every applicable gate.

### WP-38: Time entries keep the hidden-child rule on current cores (SD-22)

| Release | Size | Points | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1.0 (M3) | S | 3 | WP-34 |

**Scope**

Owner decision of 2026-11-03 (SD-22): the current 6.1-stable and 7.0-stable validate only the custom field values a user can see on a time entry (`TimeEntry#validate_custom_field_values` over `editable_custom_field_values(User.current)`), so the plugin's rule for a dependent field the user cannot see or edit (UD-07, WP-09b: a parent change is refused while such a child keeps a value the new parent value does not allow) no longer runs there. Extend the WP-09b check (`lib/redmine_depending_custom_fields/non_editable_dependencies.rb`, today wired for issues and projects through `patches/issue_validation_patch.rb` and `patches/project_validation_patch.rb`) to time entries, so the behaviour is the same on every supported Redmine version whether or not core skips hidden values: the error goes on the parent field with the existing message, a hidden child is never named and its value never disclosed, nothing is cleared, and no check runs twice on cores that still validate every value (no duplicate error). Bulk edit and the context-menu wizard of time entries follow the same rule. The failing example `spec/requests/dcf_issue_flows_spec.rb` (the time-entry UD-07 case) passes on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 without changing its expectation. Mark SD-22 fixed; CHANGELOG Fixed line with its PC row.

**Files**

- `lib/redmine_depending_custom_fields/non_editable_dependencies.rb`, a time entry validation patch (new or extended) and its registration in `lib/redmine_depending_custom_fields.rb` / `init.rb`
- specs (requests for time entries, bulk edit; models), `CHANGELOG.md`, docs/specs/*.md

**Acceptance**

- Standard gate green on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 with the current stable checkouts (the SD-22 example included); npm test

**Related decisions and defects**: UD-07, SD-22

- Own verification of the orchestrator on b354bc8 (covers the OpenAI triage spec fixes of ce7dfdf as well): test_matrix.sh rspec, js, lint `2756 examples, 0 failures` on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 (the SD-22 example green on the current 6.1-stable and 7.0-stable), JS 546/546, lint SYNTAX/COMPAT/RATCHET PASS, system specs 27/27 on 5.1 and 7.0.

**Definition of done**: the per-WP report of [`large_lists_quality_protocol.md`](large_lists_quality_protocol.md) (sections 1 to 9) with observed evidence for every applicable gate.

**Delivered (decisions taken during implementation)**

- Owner rules: no version bump (`init.rb` keeps `version '0.0.16'`), no tag added, nothing to main, no workflow dispatch; `git diff ce7dfdf -- config/locales .github spec/characterization` is empty. `git diff ce7dfdf -- init.rb` shows the require of the new patch, the reworded WP-09b comment and `TimeEntry.prepend RedmineDependingCustomFields::Patches::TimeEntryValidationPatch` next to the Issue and Project prepends. Rollback is reverting the WP-38 changes (no migration, setting, route or locale key).
- Observed core behaviour (the base of the design, `spec/requests/dcf_time_entry_hidden_children_spec.rb`, red-first runs and the probes of this WP): 6.1-stable `e79efdc` and 7.0-stable `eed6a02` define `TimeEntry#validate_custom_field_values` (`editable_custom_field_values(User.current)` only; editable is visible on a time entry), and their `TimeEntry#safe_attributes=` restores the values the user cannot see; 5.1-stable and 6.0-stable inherit acts_as_customizable's method (every value), and their `TimeEntry#safe_attributes=` removes the values the user cannot see from `custom_field_values` (`delete_if`), so the form, bulk edit, the context menu and REST never validate them and the save deletes their rows (`self.custom_values = target_custom_values`). Before WP-38: 5.1 refused a direct assignment with 'is invalid' under the hidden child and accepted the same change through `safe_attributes=`, REST (204) and bulk edit (302), deleting the hidden value; 7.0 accepted all of them and kept the value.
- `Patches::TimeEntryValidationPatch` (new, `lib/redmine_depending_custom_fields/patches/time_entry_validation_patch.rb`): `validate_custom_field_values` calls `super`, then inside core's gate (`new_record? || custom_field_values_changed?`) `NonEditableDependencies.validate(self, User.current, custom_field_values + TimeEntryValidationPatch.dropped_values(self))`, the same gate and user as core 6.1 and 7.0. `dropped_values` builds, for each depending field with saved rows that the entry no longer lists in `custom_field_values` (what 5.1 and 6.0 `safe_attributes=` removed), a `CustomFieldValue` with the stored value as value and `value_was`; nothing on 6.1 and 7.0 and nothing for a new entry (no saved row; 5.1 and 6.0 do not save such a default either). No query: it reads the rows `custom_field_values` loaded.
- `NonEditableDependencies`: `validate(record, user, values = record.custom_field_values)` (the Issue and Project patches call it with two arguments, unchanged) and `failing_values(record, values)`; new `judged_by_record?(custom_value)`, true only for a value of a TimeEntry whose field `User.current` cannot see (`visible_by?(record.project, User.current)`, core's `visible_custom_field_values`). The placement and `add_once` are unchanged; on a time entry only the two hidden placements occur (editable is visible), so no time entry branch was added.
- `DependingFormatMethods#validate_custom_value`: the D1 error is dropped when `judged_by_record?` holds (checked only when the D1 check failed); the 'skip the core check' decision of a parent value that allows nothing is taken before, so it is unchanged. Only 5.1 and 6.0 validate such a value in the format (6.1 and 7.0 skip it), so there core no longer names the hidden child for the D1 rule and the plugin check gives the single error: the same result on every core without a version check or a switch (no `Redmine::VERSION` in the three files, pinned). Issue and project validation never pass a value through the format that `judged_by_record?` accepts.
- Result on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 (each example runs on all four): a parent change while a hidden child holds a value the new parent value does not allow is refused with exactly one error, `activerecord.errors.messages.dcf_hidden_child_conflict` on the parent field (en and de), nothing under the child, nothing on base, no child name or value in the errors, for a direct assignment and through `safe_attributes=`; list, enumeration and multiple children; one message for several hidden children of one parent; base (`error_dcf_hidden_child_conflict`) when the parent is hidden too, for a new value of a hidden child under an unchanged parent and for a new entry whose hidden child has a stale default under a blank parent; a parent change to a value that allows the child saves; a change of another field or of hours and comments while a hidden child holds a legacy value is valid (UD-04); a child the user can see and an administrator get core's 'is invalid' under the child name only. REST `PUT /time_entries/:id.json` and `.xml`: 422 with `["<parent> <message>"]`, no child name or value in the body, nothing saved; the time entry form re-renders with the parent message; `POST /time_entries/bulk_update` and the bulk update link of core's time entry context menu: the entry is listed in core's failure notice as '<parent> <message>: #id', the other entry saves, nothing is cleared on the refused one.
- Context-menu wizard: it exists for issues only (`ContextMenuWizardController#find_issues` loads `Issue.where(id: ids)`, one route `POST /depending_custom_fields/save`, `ContextMenuHook` hooks `view_issues_context_menu_end` only), pinned; no time entry wizard was invented. The context menu of time entries is core's and posts to bulk update, which follows the rule (pinned through its link). The issue wizard is unchanged (no file of it changed).
- Specs. New `spec/requests/dcf_time_entry_hidden_children_spec.rb` (23 examples): registration (the patch before TimeEntry's own method, `super_method` owned by TimeEntry or acts_as_customizable, one callback), no version check, the result above, both core shapes on every version (an anonymous TimeEntry subclass with 5.1's every-value method or 6.1's visible-only method under the prepended patch, each with and without the dropped hidden value: the same `{parent => [message]}`), the dropped value judged by its stored value only, a characterization of core (5.1 and 6.0 drop and then delete a hidden value on a `safe_attributes=` save, 6.1 and 7.0 keep it), REST JSON and XML, the form, the context menu and its link, the wizard routes, the query cost (no query added to a valid change). `bulk_update_dependencies_spec.rb`: new 'refuses an entry whose hidden child the new parent value does not allow, on the parent field (WP-38)'.
- Changed existing examples (annotated 'Changed by WP-38'): `dcf_issue_flows_spec.rb` 'on a time entry rejects the change for a child not visible for the user role, in en and de': `entry.valid?` false is kept; the placement assertions follow the owner decision (`errors[parent.name] == [hidden message]` in en and de, `errors[child.name] == []`, no full message with the child name); was `errors[child.name] == ['is invalid']` and `errors[parent.name] == []`; the comment of its describe block too. `dcf_non_editable_children_spec.rb` 'prepends the check on Issue, Project and TimeEntry only, before their own validate_custom_field_values' (was 'on Issue and Project only', with TimeEntry among the unpatched classes) and its header. `dcf_usage_calculator_spec.rb` static guard: one listed line added, `time_entry_validation_patch.rb: saved = entry.custom_values.select(&:persisted?)` (it reads loaded rows, no lookup by value; open input of WP-33). No flips in `spec/characterization` (unchanged).
- Docs: CHANGELOG and section 3 M3 (appended), word for word, the Fixed line 'Fixed: On time entries, a parent field change is refused while a dependent field you cannot see holds a value the new parent value does not allow, on every supported Redmine version (Redmine 6.1 and 7.0 no longer check fields you cannot see); as on issues and projects, the error is shown on the parent field without naming that field, or as a general error when you cannot see the parent field either or its value did not change. This also applies to time entry bulk edit, the time entry context menu and REST updates (HTTP 422).' with PC-78 in the compatibility register (intro sentence, the PC row and two new table rows); the PC-69 sentence 'Time entries and other objects already refused such changes.' is now 'Other objects, except time entries, already refused such changes.' in the three copies (CHANGELOG, section 3 M1, PC-69). The API line and the Upgrade notes are unchanged (the Fixed line names REST and HTTP 422; no UN row added). SD-22 marked fixed (defects table and section: status, how, the observed 5.1 and 6.0 behaviour, recommendation done). Spec set index row 12, UD-07 (the 'Other objects' bullet without time entries and a new time entries bullet; D1 row), compatibility rows (issues and projects row and section 5 row reworded, the time entry rows added), server design (QA-08, rule 3 (g) and the new (h), the behaviour row), quality protocol FM-20b, WP-09 acceptance line, README 'Dependent fields you cannot edit' (time entries, the 5.1 and 6.0 core removal of hidden time entry values and the bulk edit form of time entries that lists every field). Locales: no key added (the two existing keys reused), `config/locales` unchanged.
- Decisions on the open questions (the owner decision taken as final, the most conservative reading otherwise): (1) the SD-22 example: 'without changing its expectation' read as the refusal (`entry.valid?` false); its placement assertions changed to the owner decision (message on the parent, child never named), because the old ones named the hidden child, which the owner rules out on every version. This reading departs from the literal Scope wording, so it is put to the owner for confirmation (open input 4 below); it is not a change within 'without changing its expectation'. (2) Same result on all four versions instead of 'run nothing extra where core validates every value': the formats leave the D1 error of a hidden time entry value to the record check, so 5.1 and 6.0 give the owner's placement too and never two errors; the rest of core's checks of such a value on 5.1 and 6.0 (required, inclusion) is unchanged. (3) The 5.1 and 6.0 `safe_attributes=` removal is not undone (that would backport the core change of 6.1 and 7.0, outside the plugin's rule); the plugin judges the removed values by their stored rows so the parent change is refused through every path, and it judges only saved rows (a new entry keeps no hidden default there). (4) The Fixed line wording is this WP's (no text was given); PC-78 is the next free id. (5) No time entry wizard exists or was added. (6) The format file does not require `non_editable_dependencies` (its source must not contain 'editable', the pin 'resolves parents only through DependencyRules'); the constant is loaded by `init.rb` through the patches.
- Deviations: the Scope says the SD-22 example passes 'without changing its expectation', and its placement assertions were changed (decision 1, open input 4 for the owner); its refusal is kept. The contract 'on cores that do not skip: run nothing extra' and its test hint (5.1 and 6.0 keep core's single error under the child name) are not followed, see decision (2); the planned characterization 'core names the hidden child once on 5.1 and 6.0' is replaced by the characterization of the core removal (decision 3). Files outside the WP-38 Files list: `depending_format_methods.rb`, `spec/services/dcf_usage_calculator_spec.rb`, `README.md`, the quality protocol and the server design. The bulk edit leak check covers the errors and the flash, not the whole page: core's time entry bulk edit form lists every available field, the hidden one with its values too (`TimeEntry.first.available_custom_fields`, the same on 5.1 to 7.0), observed in this WP. `init.rb` shows the require and the comment besides the prepend line.
- Red first, against the unchanged production code (the four changed spec files; the new file then held 22 examples, the core characterization example came later and passes with and without the change): 7.0 `254 examples, 18 failures` (`7.0-rspec-subset-20261104-162340`) and 5.1 `254 examples, 18 failures` (`5.1-rspec-subset-20261104-162514`): the SD-22 example (7.0 `expected false, got true` for `entry.valid?`; 5.1 the error under the child), the registration example of `dcf_non_editable_children_spec.rb` and of the new file (NameError), the version-check pin (file missing), the new bulk example (302 instead of 200), and in the new file every refusal, base, REST JSON and XML, form, context menu and core-shape example. Passing already, as they pin unchanged behaviour: a parent change to a value that allows the child (model and REST), a change of another field under an unchanged parent, the visible child, the administrator, the wizard routes, the query cost. After the production change the same files gave 3 failures on 7.0 and 5.1, all spec defects fixed in the spec: the order of two parent messages (now `contain_exactly`) and two whole-page leak checks of the bulk edit re-render, which core fills with every time entry field (now the errors and the flash); then `58 examples, 0 failures` for the new file and the bulk file on 7.0 and 5.1 (`7.0-rspec-subset-20261104-163137`, `5.1-rspec-subset-20261104-163213`). A first full 7.0 run (`7.0-rspec-20261104-163248`, `2755 examples, 2 failures`, lint `RATCHET FAIL ... new=2`) found the static guard of `dcf_usage_calculator_spec.rb` (two new lines, rewritten so one line is listed), the format pin 'resolves parents only through DependencyRules' (the word 'editable' in a new require line of the format, require dropped) and two Style/SymbolArray offenses in new spec lines (fixed); after the fixes `243 examples, 0 failures` and `RATCHET PASS files=161 base_offenses=118 head_offenses=83 fixed=27` (`7.0-rspec-subset-20261104-164228`, `7.0-lint-20261104-164228`).

**Open input from WP-38**: (1) Core's bulk edit form of time entries (`TimelogController#bulk_edit`, `TimeEntry.first.available_custom_fields`, the same on 5.1 to 7.0) lists every time entry field, also one hidden for the user's role, with its possible values, and the plugin adds its bulk markup to it (observed: `data-dcf-field`, `data-dcf-context="bulk"`, `data-dcf-kind`, `data-dcf-parent`, `data-dcf-parent-name` on the hidden child's select); the field name and its possible values therefore reach that page whatever the validation does. Decide whether the plugin should leave such a field without its markup (as SD-15 did for the mapping) or report it to Redmine. (2) On 5.1 and 6.0 a dependent time entry field the user can see under a parent field hidden for the user is refused 'is invalid' on any `safe_attributes=` edit that assigns custom values (core removes the hidden parent, so the format reads it as not available, UD-05), observed on 5.1 with a probe (`[false, ['<child> is invalid']]`; 7.0 `[true, []]`); not changed by WP-38 (the child is editable, so the WP-09c rule for non-editable children does not apply). (3) On 5.1 and 6.0 core deletes the values of time entry fields hidden for the user on every save through the form, bulk edit, the context menu or REST (pinned by the characterization example; README says so); a parent change to a value that allows the hidden child therefore saves and the hidden value is gone there, as before WP-38. (4) The SD-22 example: the Scope and the owner's task say that `spec/requests/dcf_issue_flows_spec.rb` 'on a time entry rejects the change for a child not visible for the user role, in en and de' passes on every version 'without changing its expectation'. WP-38 kept its refusal (`entry.valid?` false) and changed its placement assertions (annotated 'Changed by WP-38'): they were `errors[child.name] == [message]` with core's 'is invalid' and 'ist nicht gültig' and `errors[parent.name] == []`, they are now the dedicated message on the parent in en and de, `errors[child.name] == []` and no full message with the child name. The original assertions require the hidden child to be named, which the owner's rule 'a hidden child is never named' rules out; the original example, run unchanged against the WP-38 code (a temporary copy of the base commit's file, removed afterwards), fails on 5.1, 6.0, 6.1 and 7.0 at `expect(entry.errors[child.name]).to eq([message])` (en) (review round 1: `7.0-rspec-subset-20261104-173449`, `5.1-rspec-subset-20261104-173459`, `6.0-rspec-subset-20261104-173549`, `6.1-rspec-subset-20261104-173558`). The new assertions are also in `spec/requests/dcf_time_entry_hidden_children_spec.rb` ('is refused on the parent field without naming the child, in en and de (direct assignment)'). Confirm the changed assertions; otherwise either the original example is restored untouched and fails on every supported version while the rule holds, or the rule is changed for time entries.

## 5. Consolidation amendments

The amendments in section 4 apply the final completeness critic (gaps 1 to 15, see [`large_lists_review_log.md`](large_lists_review_log.md) section 3). They take precedence over the original slice text wherever they conflict.
